# Test suite redesign: the dispatch list (2026-11-03)

*(Deliverable 2 of `docs/specs/2026-11-03-test-suite-redesign.md`. One block per piece of work, in order; each is a bash
command Michael pastes as is from `~/projects/agent-harness`. A spec to build is a harness run; exploration, measurement
and review are `claude` sessions whose prompt is complete in the block. The supervisor
(`docs/plans/2026-11-03-test-suite-redesign-supervisor.md`, started by D0) keeps the **Status** column below current
and tells Michael which block is next. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-11-03-test-suite-redesign.md`.)*

## Come back to these

*(Added 2026-11-05 at Michael's ask: the commands he keeps at hand and the steps that are his to do. The supervisor
keeps it current; a step leaves when its result is in the Status table or the Log.)*

**Start the supervisor** (after a piece ends, or any time to see where things stand; it reads this list and gives
the one next command):

```bash
cd ~/projects/agent-harness && claude --model claude-fable-5-1 "$(cat docs/plans/2026-11-03-test-suite-redesign-supervisor.md)"
```

**Next command:** the n8n-home half of the review surface (F3g, block `## F3g`; session 27: both v1 live stages are done, Stage 1 confirmed by Michael's paste): a Fable session in `~/projects/n8n-home`, under that project's live-staged protocol, that builds the memory card (the memory PR's verdict with Merge and Close) from the checklist the build run wrote (`docs/2026-11-10-review-surface-n8n-home.md`), rig tests first, then the deploy, and does not set `EVENT_V2`. About 2 to 4 h (no n8n-home card session is on record; the build's milestones took 1.5 to 2 h each). **Before it, by hand:** the v1 live checklist's two stages (`docs/plans/2026-10-22-n8n-events-live.md`: Stage 1 puts `EVENT_URL` in `~/.config/harness/config.sh` and tests it on the next park; Stage 2 links and enables the request inbox's path unit and tests one hand-written request), since the card only ever receives an event once `EVENT_URL` is set and only ever answers through the inbox. Nothing of n8n-home is alive or queued; this repo is idle. When the session ends, start the supervisor; after its PR, your last step is `echo 'EVENT_V2=1' >> ~/.config/harness/config.sh`, and the next run with a memory PR puts the card on your phone.

```bash
cd ~/projects/n8n-home && claude --model claude-fable-5-1 "$(cat <<'EOF'
You build the review surface's n8n-home half: the memory card on Michael's phone, from the checklist at ~/projects/agent-harness/docs/2026-11-10-review-surface-n8n-home.md (its spec, read-only: ~/projects/agent-harness/docs/specs/2026-11-10-review-surface.md, V2-03). This project's CLAUDE.md binds, its `## Live system` above all: enter a worktree first; rig tests before any deploy; the deploy only through scripts/deploy.sh from git under `flock -n /srv/n8n/live.lock`, with the snapshot and the backup window the protocol names; never read /srv/n8n/data/config; never run, call or delete anything on the live instance through the n8n MCP. Nothing in agent-harness changes: copy the fixtures the checklist's table names from that repo byte for byte (never retype them); if one is missing or does not match what the checklist says, stop and say so. Stop at once if `harness status` shows a run of n8n-home alive.

Do the checklist's §4 in its order: (1) rig tests first, in the worktree: the intake takes the v2 memory event (kind `memory`, `"v": 2`, the `memory` object) and every v1 event unchanged, against the fixtures; the card as its own sub-workflow (42 memory card, no LLM call) built per §2: the verdict line, each failed operation with its reason, Merge and Close for each PR, no actions until the ledger's memory.md exists, and Try again after a failed action; the two actions' request JSON per §3 (the id hashes the attempt, a close's note is its `why`). (2) The deploy under the live-staged protocol. (3) Not yours: `EVENT_V2=1` in ~/.config/harness/config.sh is Michael's step, the checklist's last; do not set it, do not edit that file.

Land it by PR under this project's rules. Report: the PR, the workflow ids and names, what the rig tests covered and their result, anything the checklist asked that you could not do, and the exact line Michael then adds to his config.
EOF
)"
```

**The review surface's spec command, for the record** (done 2026-11-10, PR #173): the review surface's spec (`## F3d`): a Fable session, docs only, that writes
`docs/specs/2026-11-<dd>-review-surface.md`: the memory PR's verdict (the reviewer's `## Review` lines) shown on your
phone through n8n, with merge and close as the card's two actions, as a v2 of the n8n events contract. It merges the spec
by one PR and ends with the build's `harness queue add` command and the hand steps before it. The n8n event path is not
live yet (no `EVENT_URL` in `~/.config/harness/config.sh`, no event ever routed), so the spec puts v1 going live by its
existing checklist first. About 1 to 2 h (no spec session's time is on record to go by). Docs only, so it may run beside
anything; nothing of this project is alive or queued. When it ends, start the supervisor.

```bash
cd ~/projects/agent-harness && claude --model claude-fable-5-1 "$(cat <<'EOF'
You write one spec for agent-harness: the review surface, the memory PR's verdict shown on Michael's phone through n8n, with merge and close as the card's two actions. CLAUDE.md binds (this repo's and ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md): enter a worktree before writing; never touch real runs or real state; never send a notification or POST an event; no `harness` command but status, report, parked, needs, memory, sync. Write no code. n8n-home is live: read its repo (~/projects/n8n-home) read-only for how it consumes the v1 events and writes requests; never read /srv/n8n/data/config, never call the live n8n instance or its MCP, change nothing there.

Why: since the memory reviewer run (agent-harness-2026-11-09, R1 PR #167, R2 PR #168), every memory PR is to open with a `## Review` section, one line per operation (holds, or fails with its reason). Under MEMORY_POLICY=pr (every project today) the PR is Michael's, and it reaches him only through GitHub and `harness report`: the run's one end notification goes out before the curator and the reviewer run (`finish` in lib/driver.sh). Michael's rule (journal/learning/2026-11-07-memory-curator.md §12): make approval one click. Decision 21 (a) made this its own piece, a v2 of the events contract.

Read first: docs/specs/2026-10-22-n8n-events.md (the v1 contracts: event, request, result; A1 to A3); docs/plans/2026-10-22-n8n-events-live.md (the live checklist, not yet done: EVENT_URL is unset in ~/.config/harness/config.sh on 2026-11-10, so no event has ever been routed); docs/specs/2026-11-08-memory-reviewer.md R2-02 §5 (the kind `memory`, its title, body, click and the `memory` object field, as named); journal/decisions/2026-11-09-memory-reviewer.md and journal/decisions/2026-11-09-memory-graduation.md (its F3d bullet; the overrides: a PR merged against a fail, edited or closed against a hold); lib/notify.mjs and lib/notify.sh (the kinds, the ntfy fallback); lib/requests.mjs and lib/requests.sh (the request inbox, its verbs and refusals); lib/memreview.mjs `line`; `finish` and `curator()` in lib/driver.sh; `memory_pr` and `memory_merge` in lib/record.sh; lib/memory.sh (`harness memory`, accept and decline); journal/learning/2026-11-07-memory-curator.md §10 and §12; n8n-home's CLAUDE.md (`## Live system`) and docs/workflows.md.

Write docs/specs/2026-11-<dd>-review-surface.md (today's date) in the memory reviewer spec's shape: provenance, finding, stages, one or two milestones of three or four features with the review last; for every feature the checks written first (tests/unit/ for a pure function; tests/checks/ by journal/decisions/2026-11-03-suite-tiers.md's 30 s rule; a check_budget per milestone) and the files it touches; defaults marked *default*; a Predictions table whose every row names the §10 point it moves; falsifiers. Decide, and say why:
(1) The order: v1 goes live first by its checklist (Michael's steps), then this build, then n8n-home's half. Say what this side's build does while EVENT_URL is unset (the ntfy line, as every kind today).
(2) When the memory event goes out: once per run with a memory PR, after the reviewer's verdict is written; the run's end notification unchanged; none for a run with no PR.
(3) v2: the new kind and its `memory` field carried as `"v": 2`, every v1 kind and key unchanged. Read what n8n-home's v1 workflow does with a v2 event or an unknown kind; if it would drop or break it, the harness sends the ntfy line instead until n8n-home's half lands: name that switch.
(4) The request verbs for the card's two actions (name them): what each runs (`gh pr merge --merge`; a close, with `harness memory decline` and its `--why` where a declined row is meant), each refusal with its reason in the result JSON (a PR already merged or closed, a run or PR the ledger does not know, a facts PR whose review did not run), and how a merge against a fail or a close against a hold lands in the overrides `harness memory` already counts (no second count). The rules PR's merge stays Michael's under every policy and the card is his hand: say whether the card offers it.
(5) The n8n-home half as a checklist file under docs/plans/ for a session in that project under its live-staged protocol (what its workflow reads, the card's layout, the two actions' request JSON); no code there.
(6) What it costs Michael: one card per run with a memory PR, carrying the text R2-02 §5 named, so the `harness-log.md` note, the `Memory` row and the card cannot differ.

Not in this spec: the recurrence measure (F3e), the prune (F3c), any change to the reviewer's four questions or to the graduation rule, turning MEMORY_POLICY=auto on anywhere, the live checklist's own steps (Michael's).

Merge by one PR titled "specs: the review surface" (gh pr merge --merge), delete the branch, remove the worktree. Report the spec path, the PR number, what Michael does by hand before the build (the v1 checklist's stages), and the `harness queue add ~/projects/agent-harness --spec … --brief "…"` command you suggest (milestones in series, proceed on defaults marked *default*, never park; no `--until`).
EOF
)"
```

A row's status is one of `not started`, `queued`, `running`, `ended: <run>`, `merged: PR #<n>`, `measured: <file>`,
`missed: <measure>`, `done`. The supervisor writes it with the evidence (a PR number, a findings file, a number).

## Names

*(Added 2026-11-05, session 8. One line per code and coined term, in the words the supervisor uses with Michael. Rows of
the Status table written before this table keep their wording.)*

| name | what it is |
|---|---|
| D0 | the supervisor: the session that checks what finished, updates this list and names the next command |
| D1 | the re-baseline: the suite's solo wall, CPU and node starts measured on `main` before any phase |
| D2 | phase 1, the unit tier: the pure checks moved into `tests/unit/` under one node process |
| D2x | the unit tier's diagnosis, filled in and held (never run) |
| D3 | phase 3, the tiers: every check file fast or slow, a session's `passes` on the fast tier, the full suite at verify |
| D4 | phase 2, the resident helper: one warm node process per driver and lane answers the `.mjs` questions |
| D4x | the resident helper's diagnosis, filled in and never run (Decision 1: the two misses were accepted as predictions that were wrong) |
| D5 | the tiers' lane-time measurement: the sessions' suite share of lane time, read off the resident helper run |
| D5x | the tiers' diagnosis, filled in and never run (Decision 9: the lane-time numbers were accepted as measured): a session that would have checked the measurement and said which check files the sessions' subset runs reran |
| D6 | phase 4, the slim suite: a coverage map, a trim with no coverage lost, checks pushed down into unit tests |
| D6b | the slim suite built by hand: five interactive sessions (S1, S2, S2b, S3, S4), one PR each |
| D7 | phase 5, every project: a design (no code) of which parts of this suite travel to the other projects |
| Dx | the diagnosis template: a session that says why a prediction missed and ends in re-measure, fix, or prediction wrong |
| F1 | run resilience: merges retried through an origin outage, no default end time, a red verify opens a fix round |
| F1b | F1 built by hand, one interactive session per milestone |
| F2 | fix triage: a read-only session groups a red verify's failures into issues (deferred until fix rounds show a need) |
| F3 | the memory curator: one session at a run's end curates `CLAUDE.md` from the sessions' findings (proposed) |
| F4 | the helper's health in the record: a run's `summary.json`, `harness report` and `harness-log.md` say whether a helper restarted or went off |
| C1 | the milestone before this effort that cut node starts with a memo (run `agent-harness-2026-11-03`) |
| U1 | the unit tier's one milestone (its features U1-01 … ) |
| T1, T2 | the tiers' two milestones: the runner and the assignment; then the gate (`check.sh --fast`) and the prompt |
| H1 | the resident helper's one milestone; H1-04 is its proof feature (the before and after table), H1-05 its review |
| S1–S4 | the slim suite's four milestones: the coverage map; the trim; the `checks:` line and budget; the purity moves |
| S2b | the shape session: the spec's second milestone cut in two by hand (S2 the trim, S2b the renames, splits and tier moves; Decision 10) |
| the before row, the after row | the suite's counts and its solo wall and CPU on the tree the slim suite starts from (S1 measures it) and on the tree it ends on (S4) |
| the solo method | the solo suite's command: `systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=3G /usr/bin/time -v env TEST_JOBS=4 bash tests/run.sh`, on a quiet box |
| the coverage union | every line of `lib/` and `bin/` that at least one check file runs; a trim may not shrink it |
| a mutant | one line of the harness changed on purpose (deleted, an operator flipped) to see whether any check goes red; caught if one does |
| a marker file | the file whose presence on `origin/main` tells a hand session that a milestone has landed |
| the trace switch | `HARNESS_XTRACE=<dir>`: every harness process writes the commands it runs to a file there; off unless set; what the coverage map is measured with |
| the coverage map | which lines of `lib/` and `bin/` each check file runs (`findings-and-research/2026-11-03-suite-coverage.md` and the sets beside it), measured by the slim suite's first session |
| the mutation sample | the forty seeded mutants of the slim suite's first session (seed 20261105): 26 caught, 14 survived |
| a survivor | a mutant no check turned red on |
| a gap | a survivor whose line matters: it runs, and no check asserts on what it does (nine of the 14) |
| an equivalent mutant | a survivor whose change makes no difference a check could see (five of the 14) |
| the names target | 1,243 check names or fewer after the slim suite's last session: 85 % of the 1,463 it started from (it will be missed: Decision 12) |
| the trim | the slim suite's second session (S2, PR #115): 22 checks removed, each with its successor; record `journal/decisions/2026-11-03-trim.md` |
| a successor | the check or unit test that still makes a removed check's assertions; every removal's row names one |
| a push-down | a bash check of a pure function replaced by a unit test of the same input, one for one: the count of names does not move, the check leaves a check file for the unit tier |
| a known gap | one of the four gaps the trim did not close (each needs a stub run of its own, or a move the purity session makes); listed in the trim's decision file |
| the two lane paths | the thin-evidence escalation of a verify and the lane's exit after six refused sessions (`lib/lane.sh` 147–148, 436–437): run by slow check files only since the trim |
| the 64 MB scope check | `scope.sh: a real 64M scope kills a 256 MB allocation with exit 137 and leaves no unit`, the suite's one real systemd scope (in `08-scope-sh-…`); red about once in five runs |
| the shape | the slim suite's third session (S2b, PR #117): every check file named for what it tests, no number; record `journal/decisions/2026-11-05-suite-shape.md`, with the old → new table |
| the old numbers (file 08, 13, 15, 39, 84, 86, 87) | check files as they were called until the shape: `run-scopes-services-gc`, `park-desk`, `queue-debrief` (with `weekly-budget` and `gc-tmux` cut out of it), `memlint-self` and `memlint-run`, `node-memo`, `session-gate-check`, `session-gate-driver`; rows written before 2026-11-06 16:44 use the old names |
| the name lint | a check that no check file's name is part of another's: the runner picks files by substring, so `tests/run.sh <name>` and the rerun of a red file must pick one file alone (in `suite-runner`, slow tier) |
| slow by decision | a check file under the 30 s line that is in the slow tier because someone said so, not because of its time (three today: Decision 18) |
| the daily cleaner | root's `systemd-tmpfiles --clean`, started by a daily timer at about 16:08; on 2026-11-06 it ran over 42 min at about 40 % of a core. No timing counts while it runs: a quiet box now also means `pgrep -f 'systemd-tmpfiles --clean'` prints nothing |
| the sessions' own suite share | the part of lane time coding sessions spend in suite runs they start themselves (27 % in the resident helper run, 43 % before the tiers) |
| every caller | the sessions' own suite runs plus the harness's (the baseline before the first session, the verify): 41 % of lane time in the resident helper run, 58 % before the tiers |
| a subset run | `bash tests/run.sh <name>`: only the check files whose name contains `<name>`; what sessions iterate on |
| M1–M3 | run resilience's three milestones: origin retries; no default end time; fix rounds |
| the fast tier | the check files under 30 s solo plus the unit tier: what a coding session runs (`bash tests/run.sh --tier fast`) |
| the slow tier | the rest: stub runs of whole harness runs; it runs with the full suite at verify |
| the resident helper | `lib/helper.mjs`, started once per driver and lane shell and asked through `ask` in `lib/record.sh`; `HARNESS_HELPER=0` turns it off |
| a node start | one `node` process started; the cost the helper removes (27–41 ms each) |
| file 13 | `tests/checks/13-run-2-michael-sets-desk.sh`, the large check file every exec count is taken on |
| a stub start | one `harness start` in the suite, with `tests/stub-claude.sh` and `tests/stub-gh.sh` standing in for `claude` and `gh` |
| the solo suite | `bash tests/run.sh` at 4 jobs with no other suite or run alive; its wall and CPU are the plan's two headline numbers |
| a quiet box | `harness status` shows RUNNING 0 and QUEUED 0 and no other suite runs: the condition for any timing |
| a fix round | after a red full suite at verify, the driver appends a fix feature and verifies again (at most `MAX_FIX_ROUNDS`, 2) |
| lane time | the wall time a lane spends from its first session to its verify; the suite's share of it is what the tiers cut |
| a deciding row | a row of a spec's Predictions table set in bold: a miss there goes to a diagnosis before the next phase |
| F3b | the memory curator's follow-up: the six changes Michael's answers of 2026-11-07 asked for (tier 2 as its own PR, the `.claude/CLAUDE.md` guard, the deny list, no merge after a failed body refresh, the cap checked per operation, accept and decline with a reason) |
| F3c | the prune: the three over-cap `CLAUDE.md` files cut under 120 lines by hand, one session per project |
| F3d | the review surface: the memory PR shown, and merged or declined, from the phone through n8n (a contract change, after the n8n events run) |
| F3e | the recurrence measure: whether a fact that landed in Gotchas stopped appearing in later runs' entries |
| D6x | the slim suite's diagnosis, filled in and held (Decision 20): why the solo CPU rose where a fall was predicted |
| the memory curator | one Opus session at a run's end that reads the run's entries and proposes itemized edits to the project's `CLAUDE.md`; the driver applies what the tier allows (`lib/curator.mjs`, `curator()` in `lib/driver.sh`) |
| tier 1, tier 2, tier 3 | the curator's trust levels: facts (Gotchas, project facts), which the driver applies and puts in the memory PR; conventions and workflow lines, proposals Michael merges; protected sections (live system, permissions, never-rules), text proposals only |

## Rules for this effort

*(Added 2026-11-07, session 14, from the practice of the list's blocks (F4's "no slim-suite session open at the same
time", 2026-11-05) and of Michael's sessions beside the curator and n8n runs on 2026-11-07. They add to `CLAUDE.md`
and win where they are stricter.)*

- A harness run of this project never runs beside another: the queue holds the second until the first ends
  (`decisions/2026-10-21-queue-one-run-per-project`). Queue it rather than wait to start it.
- A `claude` session that writes only specs, plans, findings or decision files (nothing under `lib/`, `bin/`, `tests/`
  or in `CLAUDE.md`) may run beside a harness run of this project, or beside another such session.
- A session that adds check names or changes `lib/` or `bin/` runs alone: not beside a run of this project, and not
  beside another such session.
- A timing by the solo method needs a quiet box: no run alive, an empty queue, the runner off, and the daily cleaner
  neither running nor due (`tests/tools/solo-measure.sh` refuses otherwise).
- *(Decision 32 (a), 2026-11-09: these bind every prompt, brief and spec this effort writes, and the supervisor's
  reports.)* **A guard names what it found** (the step, the line, the file), never a count alone, and its message is its
  repair instruction: the note, the card and a retry's prompt carry the same words.
- **A recoverable gap gets one retry told what to repair** before any card to Michael; a stronger model is for a
  judgment gap (a step the verifier could not run), not a format gap.
- **A retro proposal seen by three or more runs' retros and still `proposed`** (the record's `retro.md` carries the
  backlog's count) is reported by the supervisor once, under *Yours to decide*, as a numbered Decision.
- **A default a spec marks *default* that a session took is a Log line, not a Decision**, unless it changes a later
  piece's assumption.

## D0 · Start the supervisor

Produces: a Fable session that reads this list and the plan, finds each piece's results, verifies the numbers, and
gives you the next block. Start it any time, and again each morning; it keeps no state but this file's Status table.
The supervisor checks: nothing (it is the checker).

```bash
cd ~/projects/agent-harness && claude --model claude-fable-5-1 "$(cat docs/plans/2026-11-03-test-suite-redesign-supervisor.md)"
```

---

## D1 · Re-baseline on `main` after C1 merged, with the box quiet

Produces: §5 "Re-baseline on main" appended in place to `findings-and-research/2026-11-03-redesign-baseline.md` (solo
wall, CPU, checks, the three slowest files, node execs by class for file 13, the eval files' node starts), merged by
PR. Model: Fable (the write-up compares four sources; a smaller model would be fine for the runs alone).
The supervisor checks: the section exists with the five rows and `harness status` showed RUNNING 0 at its start; the
solo wall is within 5 % of C1-04's after row (803.8 s) at the then check count, else the box was not quiet (rerun).

```bash
cd ~/projects/agent-harness && claude --model claude-fable-5-1 "$(cat <<'EOF'
You are the re-baseline session for the test suite redesign of this repo (agent-harness). CLAUDE.md binds: enter a worktree before writing anything (the live checkout is off limits); never touch real runs or real state; never send a notification; no `harness` command but `status`, `report`, `parked`, `sync`.

Read first: docs/plans/2026-11-03-test-suite-redesign.md (§1, §6), findings-and-research/2026-11-03-redesign-baseline.md (§1, §2: the rows you add a sibling to), journal/decisions/2026-10-31-fewer-node-starts.md (C1-04's method, the one you repeat), findings-and-research/2026-11-03-exec-table/README.md and exectable.mjs (the parser for the exec counts by class).

Preconditions, in this order: `harness status` shows RUNNING 0 and QUEUED 0 (if not, stop and say so: this measurement needs the box to itself); `git log origin/main --oneline -5` shows run agent-harness-2026-11-03's C1 merged (a `C1` merge PR). Your worktree is on origin/main.

Do, in your worktree, writing outputs to your scratch directory:
1. Two solo full suites, one after the other: `systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=3G /usr/bin/time -v env TEST_JOBS=4 bash tests/run.sh > <scratch>/solo-<n>.out 2> <scratch>/solo-<n>.time` (the runner's own `wall … s` line is the wall; user+sys from time -v is the CPU; the check count from the last line; the `slowest:` line). Give the Bash call a timeout of at least 2,400,000 ms or run it in the background and wait for its end notification; never poll.
2. `strace -f -e trace=execve,clone,clone3,vfork,fork -o <scratch>/file13.strace bash tests/checks/13-run-2-michael-sets-desk.sh > <scratch>/file13.out 2>&1`, then `node findings-and-research/2026-11-03-exec-table/exectable.mjs` over it (read its README for the arguments) for node execs by class (driver, lane, cli, stub, tests/lib.sh) and by script+subcommand, and the counts of git, cat, date, bash.
3. The unit layer: `strace -f -e trace=execve -o <scratch>/pure.strace bash tests/run.sh 01- 02- 03- 04- 05- 06- 07- 11- 12- 35- 51-` and count successful `node` execs; `/usr/bin/time -v` of the same command for its CPU.
4. Append to findings-and-research/2026-11-03-redesign-baseline.md a section `## 5. Re-baseline on main (<date>, <commit>)` with: a table of the two solo runs (wall, user+sys, checks, red files, slowest three) beside C1-04's after row; the file-13 class table and top ten script+subcommand rows beside §2's; the unit layer's node count and CPU; the load average before each run; and one paragraph saying which of §1–§3's numbers moved and why (a merged C1 feature, a check count change). Keep the raw .out/.time/.strace summaries (not the raw strace) under findings-and-research/2026-11-03-redesign-baseline/.
5. Commit on your branch, push, open a PR titled "findings: re-baseline on main for the test suite redesign", merge it (`gh pr merge --merge`), delete the branch, remove your worktree.

Rules: measure, do not fix; change nothing under lib/, bin/, tests/ or prompts/. Report at the end: the two walls, the CPU, the node count for file 13 by class, and the PR number.
EOF
)"
```

## D2 · Phase 1: the unit tier (a harness run)

Produces: run `agent-harness-<date>` building `docs/specs/2026-11-03-unit-tier.md` (U1: the reporter, `00-unit-tier.sh`,
`tests/unit/`, the move, the table in `findings-and-research/2026-11-03-unit-tier.md`, the decision file, docs), merged
by the run's PR. The queue runner is going (`harness status` says so); if it is not, `harness queue start` (no `--until`: runs are not tied to a time of day, Michael 2026-11-04).
The supervisor checks: `harness report <run>` 100 % passing and merged; the findings table has the before/after rows

The incident (run agent-harness-2026-11-04): T2 was verified at 06:17 and never merged. run.log: from 06:07:42 every record-branch push failed ("push of harness/agent-harness-2026-11-04 failed", run.log only); at 06:17:54 GitHub rejected the tag push (" ! [remote rejected] run/agent-harness-2026-11-04/T2 -> run/agent-harness-2026-11-04/T2 (failure)") while harness-log.md said "T2 verified and tagged run/agent-harness-2026-11-04/T2"; merge_one's fetch failed at 06:18:01, 06:18:04, 06:18:10 ("T2: fetch of main failed; merged later": the lane's merge_own, then the driver's finish merge_milestones), and lane_done (lib/lane.sh:236) fell through to "lane T2 closed (T2 needs Michael: its merge)" with no needs record and no notification (harness needs showed 0). GitHub was back by 06:18:41. At 06:19 gc removed the run with T2 verified and unmerged (its question cards closed "by sweep: run removed"). Reading bin/harness, `harness merge <run>` on a gc'd run reaches cmd_start with no worktree and dies on "a new run needs --spec" (.harness/config.sh has no SPEC_FILES): confirm it on a throwaway run before you state it. Spec: (1) transient origin failures (fetch, branch push, tag push, gh pr create/merge) in merge_one and verify's tag are retried with bounded backoff (a few minutes in all); one that outlasts it is a `note`, opens a needs card (a new kind, its command `harness merge <run>`) and notifies through the stubbed notify; lane_done never says "needs Michael" without a card; (2) "verified and tagged" is noted only when the tag reached origin, otherwise the note says the tag is local only; (3) record-branch push failures: one note when a streak starts and one when it ends; (4) gc keeps a run that has a verified, unmerged milestone, and `harness merge` works on a run whose worktree is gone (from the local tag and the record on main).

M2 · no default end time (Michael, 2026-11-04: runs go regardless of the time of day). Today every start or continue segment ends "deadline" 8 h in (lib/config.sh:12 `HOURS=8`, lib/driver.sh:53 `DEADLINE=$(( T0 + HOURS * 3600 ))`; a deadline end never continues by itself, lib/driver.sh:348-353); the queue runner already has none (lib/queue.sh:20 `UNTIL=${1:-0}`). Spec: no deadline unless `--until` or a set HOURS asks for one (*default*: HOURS empty or 0 = none, documented in templates/user/config.sh); a run is still bounded by MAX_SESSIONS, attempts, the weekly budget and usage-limit waits, and the spec says so. Docs in the same milestone: the overnight framing and `--until` as the normal way out of README.md (:33, :42, :45, :57, :178, :182, :189, :251, :300, :343), docs/architecture.md (:284, :582, :698, :724), CLAUDE.md (:7 "This repo runs Michael's nights", :70 "Tonight's scope": Michael's request, 2026-11-04), prompts/parts/where-things-go.md:6, prompts/parts/where-things-go-readonly.md:6, prompts/draft-claude-md.md:8,14, templates/project/CLAUDE.md:22, templates/user/config.sh:29, comments at lib/queue.sh:81, lib/driver.sh:274, bin/harness:533, and memlint's night-scope message (lib/memlint.mjs:143-165: the rule stays, its words say "run scope"). `--until` stays documented as an option. Specs, plans and decision files are history: leave them; this milestone's decision file says what changed.

M3 · a red verify is fixed, not parked (Michael, 2026-11-04: a measured step toward an agentic loop). Today a verify whose full suite is red (lib/lane.sh verify: `red`, the verify_red card, variant `own` for a slow-tier red the fast gate let through, else main merged in) stops the milestone for Michael. Spec: instead, the driver appends a fix feature to the milestone (its steps name the failing checks, HARNESS_FAILED names and the output file), the lane's next session takes it, its passes stands on the failing files green plus the fast tier, then the milestone verifies again; at most MAX_FIX_ROUNDS (*default* 2) per milestone, then today's verify_red card and notification; a flake (a green rerun) never opens one; each round is a note and is counted in summary.json and `harness report` (fix rounds opened, fixed); a fix session counts against MAX_SESSIONS. No LLM decides whether to loop: the driver does, by the rule. Shape it for a later triage step (not built now): the fix feature carries an `issue` field (this milestone: one issue per red, `all`), and each round records the failing names before and after its fix in metrics.jsonl, so a triage session can later split one red into several fix features without a format change, and the rounds show whether one fix per red clears it.

Merge the spec by PR titled "specs: run resilience" (gh pr merge --merge), delete the branch, remove the worktree. Report the spec path and the `harness queue add ~/projects/agent-harness --spec … --brief "…"` command you suggest (milestones in series, proceed on defaults marked *default*, never park; no `--until`).
EOF
)"

## F4 · The helper's health in the run's record (a `claude` session, one PR; confirmed 2026-11-05, Decision 8 (a))

Produces: a run's `summary.json` with a `helper` field (restarts, off, the reasons), a `Helper` row in
`harness report`, and one note in `harness-log.md` when a helper restarted or went off; checks, docs and the decision
file with it; merged by PR. After it, nobody reads `run.log` by hand to know. About an hour, one full suite run in it.
Needs: the resident helper merged (it is); no slim-suite session open at the same time. Model: Fable.
The supervisor checks: `jq .helper` of the next run's `summary.json` (on its record branch, then on main) and the
row in `harness report <run>`; check names added and none removed; `92-resident-helper-run`'s comparison of the
helper-on and helper-off logs still there and green; no other note text changed; then closes the hand step in *Come
back to these*.

```bash
cd ~/projects/agent-harness && claude --model claude-fable-5-1 "$(cat <<'EOF'
You are the session that puts the resident helper's health into a run's record, in this repo (agent-harness). CLAUDE.md binds: enter a worktree before writing anything; never touch real runs or real state; no `harness` command but `status`, `report`, `parked`, `sync`; never send a notification.

Why: the resident helper (lib/helper.mjs, asked through `ask` in lib/record.sh) writes its only failure signal, `helper restarted: <why>` and `helper off: <why>`, with a printf into the state directory's run.log (helper_failed in lib/record.sh). run.log is not part of a run's record, so harness-log.md, summary.json, `harness report`, the retro and the debrief say nothing when a helper fails, and Michael has to grep a state directory to know. This repo's rule is that every behaviour is visible in harness-log.md.

Read first: docs/specs/2026-11-03-resident-helper.md (the `ask` function's failed read; "After the run"), journal/decisions/2026-11-03-resident-helper.md (three restarts then off; why the line is a printf: helper_failed can run inside a $( ) and under `record`, so it must never call `note` itself), lib/record.sh (helper_failed, ask, note, record), lib/summary.mjs (`build` already reads run.log; fix_rounds with its report row is the pattern to follow), bin/harness (where the summary is built and reported), tests/checks/91-resident-helper.sh and 92-resident-helper-run.sh, the summary's unit tests under tests/unit/.

Build, each check written first and watched failing:
1. lib/summary.mjs: a pure exported function that reads run.log's text and returns how many `helper restarted:` and `helper off:` lines it holds and each distinct reason with its count; `build` writes it as `helper: { restarts, off, whys }` (zeros for a run with none; a summary built before this has no field). Unit tests in tests/unit/.
2. `harness report`: a `Helper` row when the summary has the field (`0 restarts`, or the counts and the reasons); no row for an older summary, as Fix rounds does.
3. harness-log.md: one `note` when a run's segment ends with a count that is not 0, written from the driver's own shell at the end and never from helper_failed: how many restarted, how many off, the first reason, "see run.log". Nothing when the count is 0: a normal run's harness-log.md does not change, and 92-resident-helper-run's comparison of the helper-on and helper-off logs must stay green.
4. A check in the slow file 92-resident-helper-run.sh: a stub run in which a helper is killed between two questions ends with restarts of 1 or more in summary.json, the row in `harness report` and the note in harness-log.md; a normal stub start has restarts 0 and no note.
5. Docs in the same PR: docs/architecture.md (the resident helper's guard row, summary.json's fields), README.md where it lists the report's rows, journal/decisions/2026-11-03-resident-helper.md edited in place with one `Updated in place <date>: …` line, and the spec's "After the run" grep replaced by "read `harness report <run>`".

Defaults; say so in the PR if you choose otherwise: no needs card and no notification for a restart or an off (the answers are right either way, the run is only slower); the ledger row unchanged; `harness status` unchanged.

Verify: `bash -n` and `node --check` on what you touch; `bash tests/run.sh --tier fast`; `bash tests/run.sh 92-resident-helper-run`; then the full suite once (`bash tests/run.sh`, about 10 min). Before the full suite run `harness status`: if RUNNING is not 0, stop there and tell Michael instead of running beside a real run. New check names go into tests/fixtures/check-names.txt.

Commit on your branch, push, open a PR titled "helper health in the record: summary.json, harness report, one note", merge it (`gh pr merge --merge`), delete the branch, remove your worktree. Then `harness status`: if RUNNING is 0 and QUEUED is 0, run `harness sync` so the live harness has it; else say it is left. Report: the PR number, the suite's last line, the names added, each default you kept or changed, and whether the live checkout is level.
EOF
)"
```

file on main; a check seen red first, in which a stub session started by a driver whose environment has `TMUX` and

and none removed; the fast tier and the touched check files green in the PR. Then the build run's continue.

cd ~/projects/agent-harness && claude --model claude-fable-5-1 "$(cat <<'EOF'
You make one fix in agent-harness, one PR. CLAUDE.md binds (this repo's and ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md): enter a worktree before writing; never touch real runs or real state; never send a notification; no `harness` command but status, report, parked, needs, memory, sync; never run a bare `tmux kill-server` or any tmux command against a server you did not start (this fix exists because a session did). Checks first, watched failing: a driver behaviour gets a check in tests/checks/ (fast or slow by journal/decisions/2026-11-03-suite-tiers.md's 30 s rule, named for the behaviour); every new check name goes into tests/fixtures/check-names.txt (and check-names-fast.txt for a fast file), LC_ALL=C sorted. This session changes lib/ and tests/, so it runs alone: stop at once if `harness status` shows a run of this project running or queued (the stopped run agent-harness-2026-11-10 is not running; leave its state, branch and worktrees alone).

The case: run agent-harness-2026-11-10 died at 19:44:04 on 2026-11-10, 2 min 44 s into its first coding session. Its transcript (~/.local/state/harness/agent-harness/agent-harness-2026-11-10/logs/01-V1-01.jsonl, the last tool call) cleaned up a throwaway stub run with `TMUX_TMPDIR=$T/tmux tmux kill-server` in the session's own shell. The session had inherited TMUX from the driver's tmux pane (bin/harness:420 starts the driver with `tmux new-session` on the default server; scope_run passes the environment through), and tmux follows $TMUX over TMUX_TMPDIR, so the command killed the default server and the driver got a hangup: the transcript reads `Hangup`, the lane's exit file `12 lane exited unexpectedly`, and `ended` the default `driver exited unexpectedly (see run.log)`. tests/lib.sh:21 unsets TMUX and TMUX_PANE for this reason; lib/eval.sh:66 strips them with `env -u`; session() in lib/record.sh (the scope_run ... "$CLAUDE" -p call) and lib/debrief.sh:160 do not.

Make: (a) every claude session the driver or a lane starts runs without TMUX and TMUX_PANE in its environment (one place if the calls share one; say in the decision file why that place covers them all). The driver's own environment is unchanged: it still needs its pane (bin/harness:417's capture-pane after it exits). (b) A driver ended by SIGHUP records `the driver got a hangup (its tmux server or terminal went away); state is kept: harness continue <run>` as its end reason, through the same path `finish` and the EXIT trap write `ended`, and the needs card carries those words (a guard names what it found, and its message is its repair instruction). *default*: the driver still ends on a hangup; surviving one is out of scope. (c) A decision file, journal/decisions/<today>-session-env-no-tmux.md: the case, the fix, why not survive the hangup.

Merge by one PR titled "fix: a session's environment carries no TMUX" (gh pr merge --merge) once the fast tier and the touched check files are green, delete the branch, remove the worktree. Report the PR number, the check names added, and the fast tier's result.
EOF
)"

The reviewer: (1) one read-only Opus session (answer 2's review default) at the run's end, after the curator has written its operations and before the PR bodies are final, under the follow-up's deny list and with no write to the tree or the record (the driver writes); its input is the operations, the run's record (entries.jsonl, progress.md) and the tree; (2) for every operation one evidence row (ran, saw, holds) on four questions: the line is stated by the entry it cites and the incident is in the record; a replaced or evicted line has no live referents in the tree (name the grep); an eviction happens only at the cap (15 Gotchas lines, 120 lines) and a replace or delete names a real line; the line duplicates nothing in the file or in ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md (read-only); the rows are rendered as the verify's are; (3) the verdict goes into each PR's body (a `## Review` section, one line per operation: holds, or fails with the reason), the report's `Memory` row and `harness-log.md`, and the text the card carries (the ntfy line today; say which field a v2 event would carry, for F3d); (4) under `MEMORY_POLICY=auto`, only held operations merge and the failing ones are dropped with the reviewer's reason; under `pr`, the PR stays Michael's with the verdict in front of him; (5) `harness memory` gains the reviewer's columns (operations held and failed per run; Michael's overrides: a PR merged against a fail, or edited or closed against a hold) and ledger/memory.jsonl the fields behind them; rewrite §10's graduation rule from "about five merged unchanged" to the curator's agreement with the reviewer plus few overrides, with the numbers you propose and why, and say which existing rows keep their meaning; (6) the reviewer's own cost and time in the report (the curator's are there), with a bound. Defaults to mark: the reviewer skips a run with no operations; a reviewer that fails (limit, error) leaves the PR open under both policies with one note, never merges by default. Falsifiers: a reviewer that holds every operation across three runs (it checks nothing); one whose grep for referents misses a line a check depends on; a reviewer dearer than the curator; an override rate above a third (the questions are the wrong ones).

Not in this spec: the review surface through n8n (F3d), the recurrence measure (F3e), the prune by hand (F3c), anything the follow-up spec already builds.

Merge by one PR titled "specs: the memory reviewer" (gh pr merge --merge), delete the branch, remove the worktree. Report the spec path, the PR number, and the `harness queue add ~/projects/agent-harness --spec … --brief "…"` command you suggest (milestones in series, proceed on defaults marked *default*, never park; no `--until`), noting that it waits for the follow-up run to land.
EOF
)"
```

### The run (the spec session's own command, pasted by Michael from its terminal on 2026-11-08 and put here in session 17)

Paste after the follow-up run has landed, F5 and F6 have merged, and the silent un-pass diagnosis (D8) has ended with its fix merged if it found one (all change `lib/`; a run and those sessions never overlap). The brief's sentence on declined rows is Decision 34 (a).
The runner is off, so `harness queue start` follows the add. The supervisor checks, when the run ends: like F3 (every
feature passing, both milestones merged, the record PR), then the spec's Predictions table on the run's own memory PRs
(the `## Review` section in each body, the `Memory` row's review tail, `harness memory`'s `review:` tally and the `auto:`
line), the decision files' defaults, `docs/architecture.md` and `README.md` in each milestone, and the reviewer's cost
against the curator's.

```bash
cd ~/projects/agent-harness && harness queue add ~/projects/agent-harness --spec docs/specs/2026-11-08-memory-reviewer.md --brief "Build the memory reviewer spec in full, milestones R1 then R2 in series (R2's first feature after R1), three features each, the review feature last in each. Proceed on the defaults the spec marks *default*, record them marked *default* in the two decision files, never park. The follow-up run (agent-harness-2026-11-08) is merged: read its two decision files first and use the names it built for the rules branch, memory-result.json's numbering, S_DENY/CURATOR_DENY and apply's answers argument where the spec names them. The reviewer writes nothing but memory-review.json; under auto a facts PR merges only when the review ran and every committed op held. Its duplicate question also reads the declined rows of ledger/memory-answers.jsonl, read-only, so a declined rule a curator restates under ## Gotchas fails review. memory-review.sh is a new slow check file: time it once alone and add its row under the tiers decision's table." && harness queue start
```

## D8 · The silent un-pass: a diagnosis (a `claude` session; Decision 28 (a))

Produces: `findings-and-research/2026-11-<dd>-silent-unpass-diagnosis.md`, merged by PR: the cause with evidence and one
outcome, `fix` (a spec the supervisor turns into the next block) or `not a fault` (the flip is right; only its missing note
is to build). Model: Fable. Needs: F6 merged and no run of this project alive (it writes docs only, but its stub runs load
the box). The supervisor checks: the file names the line that flips the claim, with a log line or a trace from a stub
run, not a guess; it ends in one outcome; a `fix` spec has its checks written first, its rule (every flip of a session's
`passes` at its end has a note naming the check) and its own prediction, and says whether it answers the retro proposal
`verify-failed-recheck`.

```bash
cd ~/projects/agent-harness && claude --model claude-fable-5-1 "$(cat <<'EOF'
You are a diagnosis session for agent-harness. CLAUDE.md binds: enter a worktree before writing; never touch real runs or real state (never read ~/.local/state/harness: if you need lines of an ended run's run.log, stop and say which lines to ask Michael for); no `harness` command but status, report, parked, needs, sync, except inside tests/lib.sh's throwaway environment; never send a notification. You write a findings file and, if the outcome is `fix`, a spec; nothing under lib/, bin/ or tests/. Your stub runs load the box: stop at once if `harness status` shows a run of this project alive or queued.

The case: run agent-harness-2026-11-07-3 (the n8n events run; its record is on main under .harness/runs/agent-harness-2026-11-07-3/). Its docs feature A4-01 passed, then failed the verify's re-check (harness-log.md 20:42: `verify A4: A4-01 failed the re-check, back in the queue (one more attempt)`). Its retry ended at 20:45 with `A4-01: suite ran 1x (120 s) in the session`, then `A4-01 · claude-sonnet-5-5 · passes=false · 2 min`, then `lane A4 closed (given up (nothing left to try))`. The retry's entry in entries.jsonl claims passes, and its metrics.jsonl row says exit 0, result success. No line between says why the claim became false: not the fast tier's un-pass note, not the feature-list repair note, not a limit. The retro's proposal `verify-failed-recheck` (retro-proposals.json) is about the same path.

Read first: the session-end path in lib/lane.sh, from a coding session's exit to its `passes=` line (how passes is read from feature_list.json, every place it can be set false, the fast-tier gate, the re-check's attempt count, the give-up rule); lib/features.mjs; how the verify's re-check sends a feature back (`failed the re-check`); the check files that drive a re-check and a retry (grep tests/checks/ for `failed the re-check`); docs/architecture.md's lane section; the effort's rules for a guard (docs/plans/2026-11-03-test-suite-redesign-dispatch.md, *Rules for this effort*: a guard names what it found; a recoverable gap gets one told retry before a card).

Do: (1) reproduce it with the stub in a throwaway stub run: a verify that fails one feature's re-check, then a retry whose session claims passes with a green fast tier; confirm the claim flips with no note, or say it does not reproduce and what differs from the case; (2) find the line that flips it, with evidence (the stub run's harness-log.md and run.log in its throwaway state, a trace with HARNESS_XTRACE, a diff); (3) write findings-and-research/2026-11-<dd>-silent-unpass-diagnosis.md: the case, what you ran, the cause with its evidence, and exactly one outcome. `fix`: a spec in docs/specs/2026-11-<dd>-silent-unpass-fix.md that a fresh session can build: finding, choice with defaults marked *default*, checks written first and named for the behaviour (tests/unit/ for a pure function; tests/checks/ by journal/decisions/2026-11-03-suite-tiers.md's 30 s rule), its own prediction; the rule it lands: every flip of a session's passes at its end has a note in harness-log.md naming the check, and a feature the re-check sent back is never given up without its reason in the note and the card; say whether it answers verify-failed-recheck, and whether it is one `claude` session or a harness run. `not a fault`: why the flip is right, and the missing note specified the same way. Do not build the fix.

Commit on your branch, push, open a PR titled "findings: the silent un-pass after a re-check", merge it, delete the branch, remove your worktree. Report the cause in two sentences, the outcome, the PR number, and the fix's shape.
EOF
)"
```

Read first: docs/specs/2026-11-03-test-suite-redesign.md (Phase 5), docs/plans/2026-11-03-test-suite-redesign.md (§4 Phase 5, §6), the four phase specs under docs/specs/2026-11-03-*.md and their decision files under journal/decisions/ (what was built: the unit tier, the tiers and CHECK_FAST_CMD, the helper, the checks: line and check_budget), journal/learning/2026-10-30-test-engineering.md (entries 7, 8, 10), tests/run.sh and tests/lib.sh (what a bash suite kit would carry), templates/project/ (what a project already gets). Then each case, read-only: ~/projects/pathways (CLAUDE.md, .harness/config.sh, tests/), ~/projects/dj-crate-streaming (CLAUDE.md with its ## Live system: binding; .harness/config.sh; tests/), ~/projects/hit_list (CLAUDE.md, .harness/config.sh: `npx expo lint && npx tsc --noEmit && npx jest --silent`; its jest config), ~/projects/n8n-home (CLAUDE.md with ## Live system: binding; tests/checks, tests/fakes, tests/lib.sh). Also findings-and-research/2026-10-30-run-time-budget-dj-hitlist.md (dj's suite 59 % of lane time at 580–930 s; hit_list's 17 % at 8–30 s).

Write docs/specs/2026-11-<dd>-every-project-testing.md (today's date), under 200 lines: (1) for each of the four projects, its suite today (runner, size, time if known, share of lane time if measured) and its live-system rules that bind any change; (2) which parts of agent-harness's suite travel and how: the bash runner with tiers and conformance as a kit under templates/project/tests/ for bash suites (n8n-home), the tier convention for native runners (pytest markers and -m, jest projects or testPathPattern, bun/vitest for pathways: name the exact subset command per project as its CHECK_FAST_CMD), the stubs' "mirror the live API" rule as a CLAUDE.md line, the per-run counts and the checks: line the harness already gives; (3) one shared runner or one per language: answer with a reason; (4) the rules that keep each suite growing well: tiers from day one, a count in every run's log, a trim pass per iteration, a budget per milestone, a pure function gets a unit test; (5) per project, the first step and its cost (dj: a read-only `pytest --durations=25` profile is the first step of any dj night; hit_list: nothing until its suite is over a minute; pathways: …; n8n-home: the kit), and what Michael must decide before any of it is queued. Cite file paths for every claim about a project.

Commit on your branch, push, open a PR titled "specs: every project's testing, the phase 5 design", merge it, delete the branch, remove your worktree. Report: the file path, the runner answer in one line, and the PR number.
EOF
)"
```

---

## Dx · A diagnosis before the next phase (template; the supervisor fills the brackets)

Produces: `findings-and-research/2026-11-<dd>-<phase>-diagnosis.md`, merged by PR: what the data says about the miss,
the cause with evidence, and one of: a fix spec section (appended to the phase's spec, for a `harness queue add` the
supervisor then writes as a new block), a re-measure (the box was not quiet), or "the prediction was wrong, the
measure stands" with the corrected prediction. Model: Fable.
The supervisor checks: the file names a cause with evidence (a number, a log line, a diff), not a guess, and ends in one
of the three outcomes; then the supervisor adds the follow-up block to this list under the phase's row.

```bash
cd ~/projects/agent-harness && claude --model claude-fable-5-1 "$(cat <<'EOF'
You are a diagnosis session for the test suite redesign of this repo (agent-harness). CLAUDE.md binds: enter a worktree before writing anything; never touch real runs or real state; no `harness` command but `status`, `report`, `parked`, `sync`; never send a notification. Read-only on every input; the state dir of an ENDED run may be read as findings-and-research/2026-10-29-run-time-budget.md's method does.

The miss: phase [N] ([spec file]) predicted [measure] = [predicted value] (its Predictions table) and the run [run id] measured [measured value] ([findings file], [table or section]).

Read first: the phase's spec and its Predictions table; the findings file with the measured row and the raw files beside it; the run's decision file(s) under journal/decisions/ and `harness report [run id] --md`; docs/plans/2026-11-03-test-suite-redesign.md §4 (the phase's row and the one after it); findings-and-research/2026-11-03-redesign-baseline.md (the baseline the prediction was made against).

Do: (1) check the measurement itself (was the box quiet: the load average and `harness status` the session recorded; the same tree before and after; the same check count or the difference accounted); (2) if the measurement stands, find the cause in the code or the run with evidence (a profile, an exec count, a diff, a log line: run `strace`, `time -v`, the exec-table parser or a subset of the suite yourself, in your worktree, never a real run); (3) write findings-and-research/2026-11-<dd>-[phase]-diagnosis.md: the miss, what you checked, the cause with its evidence, and exactly one outcome: `re-measure` (why the number is not trustworthy and the exact command to repeat), `fix` (a spec section appended in place to the phase's spec under `## Fix after [run id]`, buildable by a fresh session: finding, choice with defaults, checks written first, its own prediction), or `prediction wrong` (the corrected prediction and why the gain is bounded there). Do not build the fix.

Commit on your branch, push, open a PR titled "findings: [phase] diagnosis after [run id]", merge it, delete the branch, remove your worktree. Report: the cause in two sentences, the outcome, and the PR number.
EOF
)"
```
